python3\n\"\"\"\nConvert the Majnik et al. 2025 (Track2p, eLife 14:RP107540) longitudinal barrel-cortex\n2-photon dataset into the decoder-compatible pickle format.\n\nUsage\n-----\n    python -u convert_data.py <outpicklefile> [--full | --sample] [--show-processing]\n\nOptions\n-------\n    --full            process all sessions (default)\n    --sample          process only 2 sessions (for quick testing)\n    --show-processing plot every processing step for up to 2 sessions to\n                      processing_<session_id>.png\n\nDecoder task\n------------\n    input  : time elapsed from the beginning of the session, in seconds (time-varying)\n    output : motion energy discretised into 5 equal-percentile (quintile) bins,\n             percentiles computed per session (time-varying)\n    trials : consecutive, non-overlapping 60 s blocks of the session\n\nProcessing (matches the reference paper/code, see CONVERSION_NOTES.md)\n----------------------------------------------------------------------\n    neural  : F.npy (already restricted to Track2p-tracked, iscell>0.5 ROIs)\n              -> dF/F = F - maximin_baseline(F)      [track2p/gui/data_management.py:185]\n              -> non-overlapping mean of 10 frames   [Methods, \"Decoding\"]\n    motion  : motion_energy_glob.npy mapped onto imaging frames (dropped camera frames\n              become NaN) -> non-overlapping nan-mean of 10 frames -> per-session quintiles\n\"\"\"\n\nimport argparse\nimport os\nimport pickle\nimport sys\nimport time\n\nimport numpy as np\nfrom scipy.ndimage import gaussian_filter, minimum_filter1d, maximum_filter1d\n\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Constants\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nDATA_ROOT = '/app/data'\n\nFS = 30.0                    # imaging / camera rate (Hz); ops['fs'] == 30 for every session\nBIN_FRAMES = 10              # \"averaging in bins of 10 consecutive timestamps\" (Methods)\nBIN_SIZE_S = BIN_FRAMES / FS         # 0.3333... s\nBIN_SIZE_MS = BIN_SIZE_S * 1000.0    # 333.33 ms\nTRIAL_LEN_S = 60.0                   # \"Split sessions into 60-second trials\"\nBINS_PER_TRIAL = int(round(TRIAL_LEN_S / BIN_SIZE_S))   # 180\n\nN_QUANTILES = 5              # \"discretized into five equal-percentile bins\"\n\n# Suite2p default baseline parameters (track2p GUI F_processing defaults)\nNEUCOEFF = 0.0               # reference code calls F_processing without neucoeff -> 0.0\nSIG_BASELINE = 10.0          # frames\nWIN_BASELINE = 60.0          # seconds\n\n# Mouse id -> letter used in the paper (alphabetical order, see data/README.md) and the\n# postnatal day of the first recording day (Fig. 5B of the paper).\nSUBJECT_INFO = {\n    'jm031': ('A', 7),\n    'jm032': ('B', 7),\n    'jm038': ('C', 8),\n    'jm039': ('D', 8),\n    'jm040': ('E', 9),\n    'jm046': ('F', 8),\n}\n\nBRAIN_REGION = 'S1'          # barrel cortex, layer 2/3\n\n\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Loading\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\ndef list_sessions(data_root=DATA_ROOT):\n    \"\"\"Return [(subject, session_name, session_dir), ...] in subject/chronological order.\"\"\"\n    sessions = []\n    for subject in sorted(SUBJECT_INFO.keys()):\n        subj_dir = os.path.join(data_root, subject)\n        for sess_dir in sorted(f.path for f in os.scandir(subj_dir) if f.is_dir()):\n            sessions.append((subject, os.path.basename(sess_dir), sess_dir))\n    return sessions\n\n\ndef load_traces(session_dir):\n    \"\"\"Raw fluorescence of the tracked cells, (n_neurons, n_frames).\n\n    Same as `load_traces` in the dataset's own load_data.ipynb.\n    \"\"\"\n    return np.load(os.path.join(session_dir, 'suite2p', 'plane0', 'F.npy'))\n\n\ndef load_fs(session_dir):\n    \"\"\"Imaging rate from ops.npy.\"\"\"\n    ops = np.load(os.path.join(session_dir, 'suite2p', 'plane0', 'ops.npy'),\n                  allow_pickle=True).item()\n    return float(ops['fs'])\n\n\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Neural processing\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\ndef f_processing(F, Fneu=None, fs=FS, neucoeff=NEUCOEFF, baseline='maximin',\n                 sig_baseline=SIG_BASELINE, win_baseline=WIN_BASELINE,\n                 prctile_baseline=8.0, return_baseline=False):\n    \"\"\"dF/F as computed by the reference code.\n\n    Verbatim port of `DataManagement.F_processing`\n    (/app/code/track2p/gui/data_management.py:185), which is the reference\n    implementation of the paper's \"baseline corrected fluorescence traces ... (using\n    the default Suite2p parameters)\".\n    \"\"\"\n    Fc = F.astype(np.float32, copy=True)\n    if neucoeff:\n        Fc = Fc - neucoeff * Fneu.astype(np.float32)\n\n    win = int(win_baseline * fs)\n    if baseline == 'maximin':\n        Flow = gaussian_filter(Fc, [0., sig_baseline])\n        Flow = minimum_filter1d(Flow, win)\n        Flow = maximum_filter1d(Flow, win)\n    elif baseline == 'constant':\n        Flow = gaussian_filter(Fc, [0., sig_baseline])\n        Flow = np.amin(Flow)\n    elif baseline == 'constant_prctile':\n        Flow = np.percentile(Fc, prctile_baseline, axis=1)\n        Flow = np.expand_dims(Flow, axis=1)\n    else:\n        Flow = 0.\n\n    dff = Fc - Flow\n    if return_baseline:\n        return dff, Flow\n    return dff\n\n\ndef bin_mean(x, bin_frames=BIN_FRAMES, nan_aware=False):\n    \"\"\"Non-overlapping mean over the last axis in blocks of `bin_frames` samples.\n\n    Trailing samples that do not fill a whole bin are dropped.\n    \"\"\"\n    n = x.shape[-1] // bin_frames\n    v = x[..., :n * bin_frames].reshape(*x.shape[:-1], n, bin_frames)\n    if nan_aware:\n        return np.nanmean(v, axis=-1)\n    return v.mean(axis=-1)\n\n\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Behaviour processing\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\ndef motion_energy_on_imaging_frames(session_dir, n_frames):\n    \"\"\"Motion energy resampled onto the imaging frame grid, NaN where unavailable.\n\n    The camera is hardware triggered by the 2-photon acquisition (Methods: \"with the\n    microscope acquisition acting as a trigger for camera frame acquisition\"), so camera\n    frame k corresponds to imaging frame k unless camera frames were dropped.  The\n    dataset README states dropped frames can be recovered from tstamps.npy; a dropped\n    frame shows up as an inter-frame interval of ~2x the median.\n\n    Returns\n    -------\n    me   : (n_frames,) float64, NaN at imaging frames with no camera frame\n    info : dict with diagnostics\n    \"\"\"\n    me_raw = np.load(os.path.join(session_dir, 'move_deve',\n                                  'motion_energy_glob.npy')).astype(np.float64)\n    n_cam = len(me_raw)\n\n    if n_cam > n_frames:\n        # Never happens in this dataset; guard so that extra trailing camera frames\n        # (acquisition stopped after the microscope) cannot shift the alignment.\n        print(f'    WARNING: {session_dir}: {n_cam - n_frames} more camera frames than '\n              f'imaging frames; truncating the trailing ones', flush=True)\n        me_raw = me_raw[:n_frames]\n        n_cam = n_frames\n\n    if n_cam == n_frames:\n        # No frames missing: one-to-one correspondence.\n        frame_idx = np.arange(n_frames)\n        n_missing = 0\n        max_gap = 1\n    else:\n        ts = np.load(os.path.join(session_dir, 'move_deve', 'tstamps.npy'))\n        d = np.diff(ts)\n        step = np.median(d)\n        # number of *extra* frame slots spanned by each inter-frame interval\n        n_steps = np.round(d / step).astype(int)\n        frame_idx = np.concatenate([[0], np.cumsum(n_steps)])\n        n_missing = int(frame_idx[-1] + 1 - n_cam)\n        max_gap = int(n_steps.max())\n        if frame_idx[-1] + 1 != n_frames:\n            raise ValueError(\n                f'{session_dir}: reconstructed camera frame indices span '\n                f'{frame_idx[-1] + 1} frames but there are {n_frames} imaging frames')\n\n    me = np.full(n_frames, np.nan, dtype=np.float64)\n    me[frame_idx] = me_raw\n\n    # motion_energy_glob[0] is identically 0 in every session: there is no frame -1 to\n    # difference against, so it is an artefact rather than a measurement.\n    me[0] = np.nan\n\n    info = {'n_camera_frames': n_cam, 'n_missing_frames': n_missing,\n            'max_gap_frames': max_gap,\n            'frac_valid': float(np.mean(~np.isnan(me)))}\n    return me, info\n\n\ndef discretize_quantiles(x, n_quantiles=N_QUANTILES):\n    \"\"\"Assign each value of `x` to one of `n_quantiles` equal-percentile bins.\n\n    Percentile edges are computed from `x` itself (i.e. per session).  Returns int labels\n    in [0, n_quantiles-1] and the edges used.\n    \"\"\"\n    edges = np.percentile(x, np.arange(1, n_quantiles) * (100.0 / n_quantiles))\n    labels = np.searchsorted(edges, x, side='right').astype(np.int64)\n    return labels, edges\n\n\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Per-session conversion\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\ndef convert_session(session_dir, keep_neurons=None, verbose=True):\n    \"\"\"Convert one session.\n\n    Returns a dict with the per-trial neural/input/output lists and diagnostics.\n    \"\"\"\n    t0 = time.time()\n\n    F = load_traces(session_dir)\n    fs = load_fs(session_dir)\n    if fs != FS:\n        raise ValueError(f'{session_dir}: unexpected fs={fs}')\n    n_neurons_all, n_frames = F.shape\n\n    if keep_neurons is not None:\n        F = F[keep_neurons]\n    n_neurons = F.shape[0]\n    t_load = time.time() - t0\n\n    # ---- neural: dF/F then 10-frame binning -------------------------------------\n    t1 = time.time()\n    dff, baseline = f_processing(F, fs=fs, return_baseline=True)\n    dff_binned = bin_mean(dff).astype(np.float32)            # (n_neurons, n_bins)\n    t_neural = time.time() - t1\n\n    # ---- behaviour: motion energy on imaging frames, then 10-frame binning -------\n    t2 = time.time()\n    me_frames, me_info = motion_energy_on_imaging_frames(session_dir, n_frames)\n    me_binned = bin_mean(me_frames, nan_aware=True)           # (n_bins,)\n    if np.any(np.isnan(me_binned)):\n        # A whole 10-frame bin without a single camera frame. Does not occur in this\n        # dataset (all gaps are single frames) but handle it rather than crash.\n        bad = np.isnan(me_binned)\n        good = ~bad\n        me_binned[bad] = np.interp(np.flatnonzero(bad), np.flatnonzero(good),\n                                   me_binned[good])\n        me_info['n_interpolated_bins'] = int(bad.sum())\n    else:\n        me_info['n_interpolated_bins'] = 0\n\n    me_labels, me_edges = discretize_quantiles(me_binned)\n    t_behav = time.time() - t2\n\n    # ---- trials: consecutive non-overlapping 60 s blocks -------------------------\n    n_bins = dff_binned.shape[1]\n    assert me_binned.shape[0] == n_bins\n    n_trials = n_bins // BINS_PER_TRIAL\n    n_dropped_bins = n_bins - n_trials * BINS_PER_TRIAL\n\n    # time (s) elapsed from session start at the centre of each bin\n    bin_centre_s = (np.arange(n_bins) * BIN_FRAMES + (BIN_FRAMES - 1) / 2.0) / fs\n\n    neural, inputs, outputs = [], [], []\n    for k in range(n_trials):\n        sl = slice(k * BINS_PER_TRIAL, (k + 1) * BINS_PER_TRIAL)\n        neural.append(np.ascontiguousarray(dff_binned[:, sl]))\n        inputs.append(bin_centre_s[sl][None, :].astype(np.float32))\n        outputs.append(me_labels[sl][None, :].astype(np.int64))\n\n    out = {\n        'neural': neural,\n        'input': inputs,\n        'output': outputs,\n        'n_neurons': n_neurons,\n        'n_neurons_all': n_neurons_all,\n        'n_frames': n_frames,\n        'n_bins': n_bins,\n        'n_trials': n_trials,\n        'n_dropped_bins': n_dropped_bins,\n        'me_edges': me_edges,\n        'me_info': me_info,\n        'class_fractions': np.bincount(me_labels, minlength=N_QUANTILES) / n_bins,\n        'timing': {'load': t_load, 'neural': t_neural, 'behaviour': t_behav,\n                   'total': time.time() - t0},\n        # kept only for --show-processing\n        '_raw': {'F': F, 'baseline': baseline, 'dff': dff, 'dff_binned': dff_binned,\n                 'me_frames': me_frames, 'me_binned': me_binned,\n                 'me_labels': me_labels, 'bin_centre_s': bin_centre_s},\n    }\n    if verbose:\n        print(f'    {os.path.basename(session_dir)}: {n_neurons} neurons, '\n              f'{n_frames} frames -> {n_bins} bins -> {n_trials} trials | '\n              f'missing camera frames: {me_info[\"n_missing_frames\"]} '\n              f'({100 * (1 - me_info[\"frac_valid\"]):.2f}% of frames) | '\n              f'class fractions {np.round(out[\"class_fractions\"], 3)} | '\n              f'{out[\"timing\"][\"total\"]:.2f}s', flush=True)\n    return out\n\n\ndef find_bad_neurons(subject_sessions):\n    \"\"\"Boolean mask of tracked ROIs that are identically zero on at least one day.\n\n    Such ROIs fell outside the imaged FOV on that day; Suite2p returns an all-zero trace.\n    They are dropped for every session of the subject so that the tracked population\n    stays matched across days.\n    \"\"\"\n    bad = None\n    for _, _, sess_dir in subject_sessions:\n        F = np.load(os.path.join(sess_dir, 'suite2p', 'plane0', 'F.npy'), mmap_mode='r')\n        b = np.asarray(F).std(axis=1) == 0\n        bad = b if bad is None else (bad | b)\n    return bad\n\n\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Plotting of the processing steps\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\ndef plot_processing(session_id, res, outdir='/app'):\n    import matplotlib\n    matplotlib.use('Agg')\n    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt\n\n    raw = res['_raw']\n    F, base, dff = raw['F'], raw['baseline'], raw['dff']\n    dffb, meb, lab = raw['dff_binned'], raw['me_binned'], ax[5].set_xlabel('motion energy quintile'); ax[5].set_ylabel('fraction of bins')\n\n    # 7) reassembled trials: check for temporal alignment / no gaps at trial borders\n    t_cat = ax[6].set_xlabel('time from session start (s)'); ax[6].set_ylabel('quintile')\n    ax[6].legend(loc='upper right')\n\n    for a in ax[:6]:\n        a.set_xlabel('time from session start (s)')\n\n    fig.tight_layout()\n    path = os.path.join(outdir, f'processing_{session_id}.png')\n    fig.savefig(path, dpi=110)\n    plt.close(fig)\n    print(f'    saved {path}', flush=True)\n\n\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Main\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\ndef main():\n    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__,\n                                 formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter)\n    ap.add_argument('outfile', type=str, help='output pickle ap.parse_args()\n\n    t_start = time.time()\n    all_sessions = list_sessions()\n    print(f'Found {len(all_sessions)} sessions from '\n          f'{len(set(s[0] for s in all_sessions))} subjects.', flush=True)\n\n    if args.sample:\n        # one 20-min session (jm031) and one 30-min session (jm039) so that both\n        # session lengths and a session with dropped camera frames are exercised\n        wanted = [('jm031', '2023-10-22_a'), ('jm039', '2024-05-06_a')]\n        sessions = [s for s in all_sessions if (s[0], s[1]) in wanted]\n        print(f'--sample: processing {len(sessions)} Here trials are 60 s blocks imposed by the decoder task; the only curation is dropping a\n   165→trailing incomplete block (in practice there is none: 36000 and 54000 frames are both exact multiples\n   166→of 1800 frames = 60 s).\n   167→\n   168→### Decoders Trained\n   169→| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n   170→|---|---|\n   171→| Mouse motion (motion energy), same-day, ridge regression | Reported as **R²**, not accuracy. Fig. 7C/G/H: near 0 for early ages (≤P11), rising to ≈0.3–0.6 for late ages (>P11), with strong across-mouse variability. |\n   172→| Mouse motion, cross-day | Similar magnitude for late→late; near 0 involving early days. |\n   173→\n   174→The paper therefore provides **no classification accuracy** to compare against directly. The\n   175→usable quantitative expectations are (i) the R² trend with age, and (ii) the correlation between the\n   176→first PC of neural activity and motion, which rises steeply after P11 (Fig. 7D). Both are used as\n   177→sanity checks below.\n   178→\n   179→---\n   180→\n   181→## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n   182→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   183→\n   184→### Discrepancies Found\n   185→| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Paper says | Resolution |\n   186→|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n   187→| Session duration | — | 36000 frames (20 min) for jm031/jm032; 54000 frames (30 min) for jm038/jm039/jm040/jm046 | \"each session lasted 20 minutes\" | The Methods statement is only correct for 2 of 6 mice. Use the actual frame count per session. Splitting into 60 s trials handles both lengths (20 vs 30 trials/session). No data discarded. |\n   188→| Neurons per mouse | — | 221, 370, 685, 746, 541, 435 (mean 499.7) | Fig. 5B: 285, 376, 799, 728, 541, 411 (mean 523.3); text \"526 ± 190\" | The released Track2p output differs slightly from the version used for the figures (mouse E matches exactly, B is within 6, D is *higher* in the data). Most plausibly the deposited data were regenerated with a slightly different Track2p run/curation. The released data is the only available source, so it is used as-is; the discrepancy (−5 % in the mean) is documented rather than \"fixed\". |\n   189→| dF/F definition | `F_processing`: `Fc - maximin_baseline(Fc)`, `neucoeff=0.0` | — | \"baseline corrected fluorescence traces as our dF/F (using the default Suite2p parameters)\" | Consistent: \"baseline corrected\" = baseline *subtracted*. Followed the reference code exactly, including no neuropil subtraction (see Step 5 decision 2). |\n   190→| Camera/imaging sync | — | `len(motion_energy) < n_frames` in 6/41 sessions; timestamp gaps of exactly 2× the median inter-frame interval | \"the microscope acquisition acting as a trigger for camera frame acquisition\"; README: missing frames recoverable from `tstamps.npy` | Reconstructed the imaging-frame index of every camera frame from the timestamp gaps. **Verified for all 41 sessions**: `n_camera_frames + n_detected_gaps == n_imaging_frames` in every session where frames were actually dropped (6/6), and the 35 sessions with `len(ME) == n_frames` are mapped one-to-one. |\n   191→| iscell threshold | `iscell_thr = 0.50` | `iscell[:,0]` all 1, `iscell[:,1]` min ≈ 0.50 | \"default threshold of 0.5\" | Fully consistent; already applied in the released data. |\n   192→\n   193→Additional consistency checks performed before writing the converter:\n   194→- **Mice marked `*` in Fig. 5B (C, D, F) ↔ `ground_truth.csv` present for jm038, jm039, jm046.** ✔\n   195→- **PC1-vs-motion correlation rises with age** (paper Fig. 7D): e.g. jm031 (P7→P13):\n   196→  0.19, 0.12, 0.09, 0.15, 0.28, 0.38, 0.43; jm046 (P8→P14): 0.01, 0.04, 0.04, 0.01, 0.20, 0.79, 0.69;\n   197→  jm040 (P9→P14): 0.05, 0.03, 0.07, 0.09, 0.40, 0.68. Steep rise after P11 for most mice. ✔\n   198→- **Neural/behaviour lag**: cross-correlating binned population dF/F with binned motion energy peaks at\n   199→  a lag of −1 to −4 bins (neural activity ~0.3–1.3 s *after* motion), consistent with GCaMP8m kinetics.\n   200→  There is no gross (tens of seconds) misalignment. ✔\n   201→\n   202→---\n   203→\n   204→## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n   205→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   206→\n   207→### Variable Mapping\n   208→| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n   209→|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n   210→| `<subj>/<sess>/suite2p/plane0/F.npy` | `neural` | (1) maximin baseline subtraction → dF/F; (2) non-overlapping mean of 10 frames; (3) reshape into 180-bin (60 s) trials | `load_traces` (load_data.ipynb), `DataManagement.F_processing` (`track2p/gui/data_management.py:185`) | float32, shape (n_neurons, 180) per trial |\n   211→| `ops['fs']`, `F.shape[1]` | timing | bin size = 10/30 s = 333.333 ms | — | fs = 30 Hz in every session |\n   212→| bin index | `input[0]` = `time_from_session_start_s` | `t = (bin*10 + 4.5)/30` s (centre of the 10-frame bin) | — | time-varying, shape (1, 180) |\n   213→| `move_deve/motion_energy_glob.npy` (+ `tstamps.npy`) | `output[0]` = `motion_energy_quintile` | (1) map camera frames → imaging frames, dropped frames = NaN; (2) frame 0 = NaN (artefact); (3) non-overlapping nan-mean of 10 frames; (4) per-session quintile bins (20/40/60/80th percentiles) → labels 0–4 | README of dataset; Methods \"Preprocessing videography\" & \"Decoding\" | int, time-varying, shape (1, 180) |\n   214→| subject folder name | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | — | — | 6 subjects |\n   215→| — | `brain_regions`, `brain_region_idx` | all neurons = \"S1\" (barrel cortex, L2/3) | Methods | single region |\n   216→\n   217→### Key Decisions\n   218→1. **Neural signal = baseline-subtracted fluorescence (\"dF/F\" as defined in the paper).** The paper\n   219→   explicitly states dF/F was used for all analyses including decoding, and the only dF/F implementation\n   220→   in the reference code is `F_processing`, which subtracts a Suite2p maximin baseline\n   221→   (`gaussian_filter(sigma=10 frames)` → `minimum_filter1d(60 s)` → `maximum_filter1d(60 s)`).\n   222→   I reimplement that function verbatim. I do **not** divide by F0: the paper says \"baseline *corrected*\"\n   223→   and the reference code does not divide. (Checked empirically: dividing by F0 does not improve the\n   224→   PC1–motion correlation; on several sessions it makes it worse.)\n   225→2. **No neuropil subtraction (`neucoeff = 0`).** This is what the reference `F_processing` does when\n   226→   called by the Track2p GUI (line 90 passes no `neucoeff`). Empirically, `neucoeff=0.7` (the Suite2p\n   227→   default stored in `ops`) gives comparable or slightly noisier PC1–motion correlations; following the\n   228→   paper's own code is the better-justified choice.\n   229→3. **Bin size 333.33 ms (10 frames).** Directly from the Methods: \"For all decoding analysis we slightly\n   230→   denoised the dF/F as well as the behaviour traces by averaging in bins of 10 consecutive timestamps.\"\n   231→   Same binning is applied to the behavioural trace, as the Methods require.\n   232→4. **Trials = consecutive non-overlapping 60 s blocks** (180 bins), as required by the Decoder Task.\n   233→   This mirrors the paper's own use of consecutive time blocks (2 min) for cross-validation splits.\n   234→   36000 and 54000 frames are exact multiples of 1800, so no partial trial is ever discarded.\n   235→5. **Missing camera frames** are reconstructed, not ignored: camera frame *k*'s imaging-frame index is\n   236→   `k + (number of dropped frames before k)`, where drops are detected as inter-frame intervals ≈ 2× the\n   237→   median. Motion energy at dropped frames is NaN and excluded from the bin mean (nan-mean). Dropped\n   238→   frames are never consecutive (all gaps are exactly one frame), so no 10-frame bin is ever fully\n   239→   missing. Sessions where `len(ME) == n_frames` use the identity mapping (this also covers 3 jm046\n   240→   sessions that contain timestamp jitter but no actually-missing frames).\n   241→6. **Motion energy at frame 0 is treated as missing** (it is identically 0 in every session because a\n   242→   frame difference needs a preceding frame). Affects at most 1 of 10 samples in the first bin of a\n   243→   session.\n   244→7. **Output discretisation: per-session quintiles of the *binned* motion energy.** The Decoder Task\n   245→   specifies \"five equal-percentile bins, selected per session\". Percentiles are computed on the same\n   246→   binned trace that is used as the target, over all bins of the session, so the 5 classes are\n   247→   (near-)exactly equally populated within each session. Per-session percentiles also normalise away the\n   248→   arbitrary per-session scale of motion energy (different illumination/camera gain/animal size).\n   249→8. **Drop the 8 ROIs that are identically zero on at least one day** (per subject, applied to all that\n   250→   subject's sessions so the tracked population stays matched across days). These ROIs carry no signal on\n   251→   those days and would contribute all-zero rows. 2998 → 2990 neurons.\n   252→9. **No session/trial exclusion.** All 41 sessions and all 1090 trials are kept: all sessions have\n   253→   ≥20 trials (≫2 required), no NaNs in F, and behavioural coverage is ≥99.6 % of frames in every session.\n   254→10. **Alignment event = start of the 60 s trial** (`off_start = 0`, `off_end = 60`). The recordings are\n   255→    continuous spontaneous activity with no task events, so the only meaningful alignment is the\n   256→    session/trial start.\n   257→\n   258→### Planned Sanity Checks\n   259→- [x] Subject↔paper-letter mapping confirmed via `ground_truth.csv` ↔ `*` in Fig. 5B.\n   260→- [x] `n_camera_frames + n_gaps == n_imaging_frames` for every session with dropped frames.\n   261→- [ ] Converted neural value at a random (session, trial, neuron, bin) equals a from-scratch\n   262→      recomputation directly from `F.npy` (`np.allclose`).\n   263→- [ ] Converted input time equals `(global_bin*10 + 4.5)/30` and is continuous across trial boundaries.\n   264→- [ ] Converted output label equals `np.digitize` of the independently recomputed binned motion energy.\n   265→- [ ] Output class fractions ≈ 0.2 each per session.\n   266→- [ ] Trial/session/neuron counts match Step 2 table.\n   267→- [ ] PC1-vs-motion correlation computed from the *converted* data reproduces the age trend of Fig. 7D.\n   268→- [ ] Neural↔motion cross-correlation peaks at small positive neural lag (no gross misalignment).\n   269→\n   270→---\n   271→\n   272→## Step 6: Script Development\n   273→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   274→\n   275→`/app/convert_data.py` implements the mapping of Step 5. Structure:\n   276→\n   277→| Function | Purpose |\n   278→|---|---|\n   279→| `list_sessions()` | enumerate the 41 (subject, session, dir) triples in subject/chronological order |\n   280→| `load_traces(session_dir)` | copy of the dataset notebook's loader (`suite2p/plane0/F.npy`) |\n   281→| `load_fs(session_dir)` | imaging rate from `ops.npy` (asserted == 30 Hz for every session) |\n   282→| `f_processing(...)` | **verbatim port of `track2p/gui/data_management.py:F_processing`** — Suite2p maximin baseline, `neucoeff=0`, `sig_baseline=10`, `win_baseline=60 s`; returns `F - baseline` |\n   283→| `bin_mean(x, 10)` | non-overlapping 10-frame means (optionally nan-aware), the paper's decoding binning |\n   284→| `motion_energy_on_imaging_frames(...)` | maps camera frames onto the imaging-frame grid, reconstructing dropped frames from `tstamps.npy`; NaN for missing frames and for frame 0 |\n   285→| `discretize_quantiles(x, 5)` | per-session 20/40/60/80th-percentile edges → labels 0–4 |\n   286→| `find_bad_neurons(...)` | per-subject mask of ROIs with an all-zero trace on ≥1 day |\n   287→| `convert_session(...)` | full per-session pipeline + timing + diagnostics |\n   288→| `plot_processing(...)` | 7-panel figure of every processing step (`--show-processing`) |\n   289→| `main()` | assembles the target dict, runs structural sanity checks, pickles the result |\n   290→\n   291→The script runs as `python -u /app/convert_data.py <outpicklefile>` with `--full` (default),\n   292→`--sample` (2 sessions: one 20-min session *with* 116 dropped camera frames and one 30-min\n   293→session, so both session lengths and the dropped-frame path are exercised) and\n   294→`--show-processing`.\n   295→\n   296→Code inefficiencies identified: none material. The only heavy operations are the three\n   297→`scipy.ndimage` filters used for the baseline; these are already O(n) (van Herk/Gil-Werman\n   298→for the min/max filters) and take ~1.2 s for the largest session (746 × 54000).\n   299→\n   300→Code speedups added: memory-mapped loading in `find_bad_neurons`; all binning done with a\n   301→single reshape+mean instead of a Python loop; trials produced by slicing the already-binned\n   302→matrix (no recomputation per trial); `float32` throughout for the neural data.\n   303→\n   304→---\n   305→\n   306→## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n   307→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   308→\n   309→Command: `python -u /app/convert_data.py /app/sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing`\n   310→(log: `/app/conversion_sample_out.txt`).\n   311→\n   312→### Sample Statistics\n   313→| Statistic | Value |\n   314→|-----------|-------|\n   315→| Sessions | 2 (jm031/2023-10-22, jm039/2024-05-06) |\n   316→| Neurons (total) | 966 |\n   317→| Neurons / session | 220, 746 |\n   318→| Subjects (used) | 2 (of the 6 listed in `subjects`) |\n   319→| Sessions / subject | 1 |\n   320→| Trials (total) | 50 |\n   321→| Trials / session | 20 (20 min session), 30 (30 min session) |\n   322→| `time_from_session_start_s` range | [0.15, 1799.82] s |\n   323→| `motion_energy_quintile` distribution | [0.200, 0.200, 0.200, 0.200, 0.200] |\n   324→\n   325→### Processing Plots Review\n   326→`processing_jm031_2023-10-22_a.png`, `processing_jm039_2024-05-06_a.png`. Checked and no\n   327→anomalies:\n   328→- Panel 1: the maximin baseline hugs the bottom of each raw trace and is flat under calcium\n   329→  transients — i.e. it is a baseline, not a smoothed copy of the signal.\n   330→- Panel 2: binned dF/F overlays the 30 Hz dF/F with no time offset and no amplitude bias.\n   331→- Panel 3: the binned raster of jm031 at P11 shows the synchronous network events described\n   332→  in the paper; Both 3600 and 5400 bins are exact\n   498→  multiples of 180, so no partial trial is ever produced and nothing is discarded; the code\n   499→  would drop a partial trial if one existed and reports `n_dropped_bins`.\n   500→- **Off-by-one at trial borders**: verified in `cache/sanity_checks.py` by concatenating\n   501→  trials and requiring a constant 1/3 s step across every border, and by checking that the\n   502→  concatenated output equals the session-level label vector exactly.\n   503→- **First/last frames**: bin centres use `(bin*10 + 4.5)/30`, so the first bin is centred at\n   504→  0.15 s (not 0 s) and the last ends exactly at the session end.\n   505→- **Motion energy frame 0** is identically 0 in every session (frame-difference artefact) and\n   506→  is excluded via NaN, so it cannot pull the first bin toward class 0.\n   507→- **Dropped camera frames**: all gaps are exactly one frame (max `n_steps` = 2), so no 10-frame\n   508→  bin is ever entirely missing; nan-mean handles the ≤1 missing sample per bin, and a fully\n   509→  missing bin would be linearly interpolated (count reported).\n   510→- **Timestamp jitter without real drops**: 3 jm046 sessions have `len(ME) == n_frames` but a\n   511→  few > 1.5× median inter-frame intervals. Using `len(ME) == n_frames` as the primary test\n   512→  means these correctly take the identity mapping instead of being spuriously shifted.\n   513→- **Camera longer than imaging**: does not occur, but is now guarded (truncate + warning).\n   514→- **Sessions with < 2 trials**: asserted impossible (minimum is 20).\n   515→- **All-zero / constant neural rows**: removed (Step 5 decision 8); `verify_data_format`\n   516→  reports no \"all neural data is zero\" warnings.\n   517→- **NaN/Inf**: none in `F.npy`; asserted absent in every converted trial.\n   518→\n   519→### Issues Found and Resolved\n   520→1. *Sanity assertion on the time step failed* (float32 resolution is ~1e-4 s at t ≈ 1800 s,\n   521→   which exceeds `np.allclose`'s default tolerance). Because 1.55× is not far above that threshold I\n   570→investigated it rather than accepting it:\n   571→\n   572→- **Is the signal real, or is it coming from the time input?** Ablation\n   573→  (`/app/cache/ablation.py noneural`, replacing the neural data with near-zero noise while\n   574→  keeping the time input) gives **0.209 validation balanced accuracy = chance**. So *all* of\n   575→  the decoding performance comes from the neural data, and the time input leaks nothing.\n   576→- **Is it limited by the neural representation?** Ablation with per-neuron z-scored dF/F\n   577→  (`/app/cache/ablation.py zscore`) gives 0.320 vs 0.309 — a change within run-to-run\n   578→  variability, and not a processing step the reference performs. Dividing by F0 was *worse*\n   579→  on the sample (0.280 vs 0.325). So the reference processing is not costing accuracy.\n   580→- **Is the ceiling intrinsic?** Yes, and the paper says so. The paper's own same-day decoding\n   581→  of this exact variable gives R² ≈ 0 for the ≤P11 sessions (Fig. 7C/H) — and 25 of the 41\n   582→  sessions are ≤P11. Splitting my accuracies the same way (0.285 early vs 0.324 late,\n   583→  p = 0.017) reproduces that structure: the modest pooled number is dominated by sessions in\n   584→  which the paper also finds essentially no behavioural-state representation.\n   585→- Additionally, the five classes are quintiles of a heavy-tailed variable, so classes 0–2 all\n   586→  lie within the narrow \"no movement\" band of motion energy (see panel 5 of the processing\n   587→  plots). Discriminating them is intrinsically harder than discriminating \"moving\" from\n   588→  \"still\"; this is a property of the required 5-quintile discretisation, not of the\n   589→  conversion.\n   590→\n   591→### Check 2 — Accuracy comparison to paper\n   592→| Variable | My accuracy | Paper's reported value |\n   593→|---|---|---|\n   594→| motion energy (5 quintiles) | 0.309 balanced accuracy (chance 0.200); 0.285 early / 0.324 late | **No accuracy is reported anywhere in the paper.** The paper decodes motion energy as a *continuous* variable with ridge regression and reports R² (Fig. 7C, G, H): ≈0 for early (≤P11) sessions, ≈0.3–0.6 for late (>P11) sessions. |\n   595→\n   596→The paper's quantity (R² of a continuous regression) is not convertible into a 5-class\n   597→balanced accuracy, so the comparison is necessarily qualitative. Overfitting at that\n   616→  parameter-to-sample ratio is expected, and I cannot change the decoder.\n   617→- The one remaining data-side consideration is that neighbouring 60 s trials are temporally\n   618→  adjacent and motion energy is autocorrelated, so a random trial split is not as strict as the\n   619→  paper's consecutive-block split. The decoder is memoryless and per-timepoint, so it can only\n   620→  exploit this through slow drift; and the trial split is performed by `train_decoder.py`,\n   621→  not by the conversion.\n   622→\n   623→### Additional debugging steps performed\n   624→1. **Output values verified against raw data for specific trials** — Check 2 of Step 10:\n   625→   exact label match for randomly chosen trials in 6 sessions, recomputed from\n   626→   `motion_energy_glob.npy`.\n   627→2. **Temporal alignment verified by cross-correlation** — binned population dF/F vs binned\n   628→   motion energy peaks at a lag of −1 to −4 bins (0.3–1.3 s), i.e. neural activity *follows*\n   629→   motion by about one GCaMP8m rise time. The dataset\n  README says the dropped-frame indices can be recovered from `tstamps.npy` / `interframe_int.npy` and\n  that the missing values can be treated as missing or interpolated over.\n- **Temporal binning**: non-overlapping means of 10 consecutive 30 Hz samples for *both* dF/F and the\n  behavioural trace → an effective 3 Hz sampling, 333.33 ms bins.\n- **Neural processing**: Suite2p maximin baseline (`sig_baseline=10` frames, `win_baseline=60` s),\n  subtracted from F.\n\n### Curation Steps\n**Neuron curation rules**: Suite2p `iscell` probability > 0.5 **and** successfully tracked across all\ndays of that mouse. Both are already applied in the released files. (I additionally drop the 8 ROIs\nwhose trace is identically zero on at least one day — see Step 5 decisions.)\n\n**Trial curation rules**: The paper has no trial structure (continuous spontaneous-activity\nrecordings). Here trials are 60 s blocks imposed by the decoder task; the only curation is dropping a\ntrailing incomplete block (in practice there is none: 36000 and 54000 frames are both exact multiples\nof 1800 frames = 60 s).\n\n### Decoders Trained\n| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n|---|---|\n| Mouse motion (motion energy), same-day, ridge regression | Reported as **R²**, not accuracy. Both are used as\nsanity checks below.\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Paper says | Resolution |\n|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n| Session duration | — | 36000 frames (20 min) for jm031/jm032; 54000 frames (30 min) for jm038/jm039/jm040/jm046 | \"each session lasted 20 minutes\" | The Methods statement is only correct for 2 of 6 mice. Use the actual frame count per session. Splitting into 60 s trials handles both lengths (20 vs 30 trials/session). No data discarded. |\n| Neurons per mouse | — | 221, 370, 685, 746, 541, 435 (mean 499.7) | Fig. 5B: 285, 376, 799, 728, 541, 411 (mean 523.3); text \"526 ± 190\" | The released Track2p output differs slightly from the version used for the figures (mouse E matches exactly, B is within 6, D is *higher* in the data). Most plausibly the deposited data were regenerated with a slightly different Track2p run/curation. The released data is the only available source, so it is used as-is; the discrepancy (−5 % in the mean) is documented rather than \"fixed\". |\n| dF/F definition | `F_processing`: `Fc - maximin_baseline(Fc)`, `neucoeff=0.0` | — | \"baseline corrected fluorescence traces as our dF/F (using the default Suite2p parameters)\" | Consistent: \"baseline corrected\" = baseline *subtracted*. Followed the reference code exactly, including no neuropil subtraction (see Step 5 decision 2). |\n| Camera/imaging sync | — | `len(motion_energy) < n_frames` in 6/41 sessions; timestamp gaps of exactly 2× the median inter-frame interval | \"the microscope acquisition acting as a trigger for camera frame acquisition\"; README: missing frames recoverable from `tstamps.npy` | Reconstructed the imaging-frame index of every camera frame from the timestamp gaps. **Verified for all 41 sessions**: `n_camera_frames + n_detected_gaps == n_imaging_frames` in every session where frames were actually dropped (6/6), and the 35 sessions with `len(ME) == n_frames` are mapped one-to-one. |\n| iscell threshold | `iscell_thr = 0.50` | `iscell[:,0]` all 1, `iscell[:,1]` min ≈ 0.50 | \"default threshold of 0.5\" | Fully consistent; already applied in the released data. |\n\nAdditional consistency checks performed before writing the converter:\n- **Mice marked `*` in Fig. 5B (C, D, F) ↔ `ground_truth.csv` present for jm038, jm039, jm046.** ✔\n- **PC1-vs-motion correlation rises with age** (paper Fig. 7D): e.g. jm031 (P7→P13):\n  0.19, 0.12, 0.09, 0.15, 0.28, 0.38, 0.43; jm046 (P8→P14): 0.01, 0.04, 0.04, 0.01, 0.20, 0.79, 0.69;\n  jm040 (P9→P14): 0.05, 0.03, 0.07, 0.09, 0.40, 0.68. Steep rise after P11 for most mice. ✔\n- **Neural/behaviour lag**: cross-correlating binned population dF/F with binned motion energy peaks at\n  a lag of −1 to −4 bins (neural activity ~0.3–1.3 s *after* motion), consistent with GCaMP8m kinetics.\n  There is no gross (tens of seconds) misalignment. ✔\n\n---\n\n## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Variable Mapping\n| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n| `<subj>/<sess>/suite2p/plane0/F.npy` | `neural` | (1) maximin baseline subtraction → dF/F; (2) non-overlapping mean of 10 frames; (3) reshape into 180-bin (60 s) trials | `load_traces` (load_data.ipynb), `DataManagement.F_processing` (`track2p/gui/data_management.py:185`) | float32, shape (n_neurons, 180) per trial |\n| `ops['fs']`, `F.shape[1]` | timing | bin size = 10/30 s = 333.333 ms | — | fs = 30 Hz in every session |\n| bin index | `input[0]` = `time_from_session_start_s` | `t = (bin*10 + 4.5)/30` s (centre of the 10-frame bin) | — | time-varying, shape (1, 180) |\n| `move_deve/motion_energy_glob.npy` (+ `tstamps.npy`) | `output[0]` = `motion_energy_quintile` | (1) map camera frames → imaging frames, dropped frames = NaN; (2) frame 0 = NaN (artefact); (3) non-overlapping nan-mean of 10 frames; (4) per-session quintile bins (20/40/60/80th percentiles) → labels 0–4 | README of dataset; Methods \"Preprocessing videography\" & \"Decoding\" | int, time-varying, shape (1, 180) |\n| subject folder name | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | — | — | 6 subjects |\n| — | `brain_regions`, `brain_region_idx` | all neurons = \"S1\" (barrel cortex, L2/3) | Methods | single region |\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Neural signal = baseline-subtracted fluorescence (\"dF/F\" as defined in the paper).** The paper\n   explicitly states dF/F was used for all analyses including decoding, and the only dF/F implementation\n   in the reference code is `F_processing`, which subtracts a Suite2p maximin baseline\n   (`gaussian_filter(sigma=10 frames)` → `minimum_filter1d(60 s)` → `maximum_filter1d(60 s)`).\n   I reimplement that function verbatim. I do **not** divide by F0: the paper says \"baseline *corrected*\"\n   and the reference code does not divide. (Checked empirically: dividing by F0 does not improve the\n   PC1–motion correlation; on several sessions it makes it worse.)\n2. **No neuropil subtraction (`neucoeff = 0`).** This is what the reference `F_processing` does when\n   called by the Track2p GUI (line 90 passes no `neucoeff`). Empirically, `neucoeff=0.7` (the Suite2p\n   default stored in `ops`) gives comparable or slightly noisier PC1–motion correlations; following the\n   paper's own code is the better-justified choice.\n3. **Bin size 333.33 ms (10 frames).** Directly from the Methods: \"For all decoding analysis we slightly\n   denoised the dF/F as well as the behaviour traces by averaging in bins of 10 consecutive timestamps.\"\n   Same binning is applied to the behavioural trace, as the Methods require.\n4. **Trials = consecutive non-overlapping 60 s blocks** (180 bins), as required by the Decoder Task.\n   This mirrors the paper's own use of consecutive time blocks (2 min) for cross-validation splits.\n   36000 and 54000 frames are exact multiples of 1800, so no partial trial is ever discarded.\n5. **Missing camera frames** are reconstructed, not ignored: camera frame *k*'s imaging-frame index is\n   `k + (number of dropped frames before k)`, where drops are detected as inter-frame intervals ≈ 2× the\n   median. Motion energy at dropped frames is NaN and excluded from the bin mean (nan-mean). Dropped\n   frames are never consecutive (all gaps are exactly one frame), so no 10-frame bin is ever fully\n   missing. Sessions where `len(ME) == n_frames` use the identity mapping (this also covers 3 jm046\n   sessions that contain timestamp jitter but no actually-missing frames).\n6. **Motion energy at frame 0 is treated as missing** (it is identically 0 in every session because a\n   frame difference needs a preceding frame). Affects at most 1 of 10 samples in the first bin of a\n   session.\n7. **Output discretisation: per-session quintiles of the *binned* motion energy.** The Decoder Task\n   specifies \"five equal-percentile bins, selected per session\". Percentiles are computed on the same\n   binned trace that is used as the target, over all bins of the session, so the 5 classes are\n   (near-)exactly equally populated within each session. Per-session percentiles also normalise away the\n   arbitrary per-session scale of motion energy (different illumination/camera gain/animal size).\n8. **Drop the 8 ROIs that are identically zero on at least one day** (per subject, applied to all that\n   subject's sessions so the tracked population stays matched across days). These ROIs carry no signal on\n   those days and would contribute all-zero rows. 2998 → 2990 neurons.\n9. **No session/trial exclusion.** All 41 sessions and all 1090 trials are kept: all sessions have\n   ≥20 trials (≫2 required), no NaNs in F, and behavioural coverage is ≥99.6 % of frames in every session.\n10. **Alignment event = start of the 60 s trial** (`off_start = 0`, `off_end = 60`). The recordings are\n    continuous spontaneous activity with no task events, so the only meaningful alignment is the\n    session/trial start.\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [x] Subject↔paper-letter mapping confirmed via `ground_truth.csv` ↔ `*` in Fig. 5B.\n- [x] `n_camera_frames + n_gaps == n_imaging_frames` for every session with dropped frames.\n- [ ] Converted neural value at a random (session, trial, neuron, bin) equals a from-scratch\n      recomputation directly from `F.npy` (`np.allclose`).\n- [ ] Converted input time equals `(global_bin*10 + 4.5)/30` and is continuous across trial boundaries.\n- [ ] Converted output label equals `np.digitize` of the independently recomputed binned motion energy.\n- [ ] Output class fractions ≈ 0.2 each per session.\n- [ ] Trial/session/neuron counts match Step 2 table.\n- [ ] PC1-vs-motion correlation computed from the *converted* data reproduces the age trend of Fig. 7D.\n- [ ] Neural↔motion cross-correlation peaks at small positive neural lag (no gross misalignment).\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Script Development\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n`/app/convert_data.py` implements the mapping of Step 5. Structure:\n\n| Function | Purpose |\n|---|---|\n| `list_sessions()` | enumerate the 41 (subject, session, dir) triples in subject/chronological order |\n| `load_traces(session_dir)` | copy of the dataset notebook's loader (`suite2p/plane0/F.npy`) |\n| `load_fs(session_dir)` | imaging rate from `ops.npy` (asserted == 30 Hz for every session) |\n| `f_processing(...)` | **verbatim port of `track2p/gui/data_management.py:F_processing`** — Suite2p maximin baseline, `neucoeff=0`, `sig_baseline=10`, `win_baseline=60 s`; returns `F - baseline` |\n| `bin_mean(x, 10)` | non-overlapping 10-frame means (optionally nan-aware), the paper's decoding binning |\n| `motion_energy_on_imaging_frames(...)` | maps camera frames onto the imaging-frame grid, reconstructing dropped frames from `tstamps.npy`; NaN for missing frames and for frame 0 |\n| `discretize_quantiles(x, 5)` | per-session 20/40/60/80th-percentile edges → labels 0–4 |\n| `find_bad_neurons(...)` | per-subject mask of ROIs with an all-zero trace on ≥1 day |\n| `convert_session(...)` | full per-session pipeline + timing + diagnostics |\n| `plot_processing(...)` | 7-panel figure of every processing step (`--show-processing`) |\n| `main()` | assembles the target dict, runs structural sanity checks, pickles the result |\n\nThe script runs as `python -u /app/convert_data.py <outpicklefile>` with `--full` (default),\n`--sample` (2 sessions: one 20-min session *with* 116 dropped camera frames and one 30-min\nsession, so both session lengths and the dropped-frame path are exercised) and\n`--show-processing`.\n\nCode inefficiencies identified: none material. The only heavy operations are the three\n`scipy.ndimage` filters used for the baseline; these are already O(n) (van Herk/Gil-Werman\nfor the min/max filters) and take ~1.2 s for the largest session (746 × 54000).\n\nCode speedups added: memory-mapped loading in `find_bad_neurons`; all binning done with a\nsingle reshape+mean instead of a Python loop; trials produced by slicing the already-binned\nmatrix (no recomputation per trial); `float32` throughout for the neural data.\n\n---\n\n## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\nCommand: `python -u /app/convert_data.py /app/sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing`\n(log: `/app/conversion_sample_out.txt`).\n\n### Sample Statistics\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Sessions | 2 (jm031/2023-10-22, jm039/2024-05-06) |\n| Neurons (total) | 966 |\n| Neurons / session | 220, 746 |\n| Subjects (used) | 2 (of the 6 listed in `subjects`) |\n| Sessions / subject | 1 |\n| Trials (total) | 50 |\n| Trials / session | 20 (20 min session), 30 (30 min session) |\n| `time_from_session_start_s` range | [0.15, 1799.82] s |\n| `motion_energy_quintile` distribution | [0.200, 0.200, 0.200, 0.200, 0.200] |\n\n### Processing Plots Review\n`processing_jm031_2023-10-22_a.png`, `processing_jm039_2024-05-06_a.png`. Reference code comparison\n| Stage | Reference | My script | Match? |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| (a) Data loading | `load_traces` in `data/load_data.ipynb`: `np.load(session_dir/suite2p/plane0/F.npy)`; |\n\nDifferences and reasons: only (f), (g) and (h), each dictated by the Decoder Task\nspecification, plus the extra all-zero-ROI removal in (b), which removes rows that carry no\nsignal at Everything that is verifiable matches except the per-mouse cell\ncounts printed in Fig. 5B, which do not match the deposited data themselves (i.e. the\ndiscrepancy exists between the paper and its own data release, not in my conversion). I\nverified this by counting rows in `F.npy` directly: 221/370/685/746/541/435 before my\ncuration, versus 285/376/799/728/541/411 in the figure. Because mouse D is *larger* in the\ndata than in the figure, no filtering choice on my side could reconcile the two.\n\n### Check 5 — Edge cases\n- **Trailing partial trial**: `n_trials = n_bins // 180`. Both 3600 and 5400 bins are exact\n  multiples of 180, so no partial trial is ever produced and nothing is discarded; the code\n  would drop a partial trial if one existed and reports `n_dropped_bins`.\n- **Off-by-one at trial borders**: verified in `cache/sanity_checks.py` by concatenating\n  trials and requiring a constant 1/3 s step across every border, and by checking that the\n  concatenated output equals the session-level label vector exactly.\n- **First/last frames**: bin centres use `(bin*10 + 4.5)/30`, so the first bin is centred at\n  0.15 s (not 0 s) and the last ends exactly at the session end.\n- **Motion energy frame 0** is identically 0 in every session (frame-difference artefact) and\n  is excluded via NaN, so it cannot pull the first bin toward class 0.\n- **Dropped camera frames**: all gaps are exactly one frame (max `n_steps` = 2), so no 10-frame\n  bin is ever entirely missing; nan-mean handles the ≤1 missing sample per bin, and a fully\n  missing bin would be linearly interpolated (count reported).\n- **Timestamp jitter without real drops**: 3 jm046 sessions have `len(ME) == n_frames` but a\n  few > 1.5× median inter-frame intervals. Using `len(ME) == n_frames` as the primary test\n  means these correctly take the identity mapping instead of being spuriously shifted.\n- **Camera longer than imaging**: does not occur, but is now guarded (truncate + warning).\n- **Sessions with < 2 trials**: asserted impossible (minimum is 20).\n- **All-zero / constant neural rows**: removed (Step 5 decision 8); `verify_data_format`\n  reports no \"all neural data is zero\" warnings.\n- **NaN/Inf**: none in `F.npy`; asserted absent in every converted trial.\n\n### Issues Found and Resolved\n1. *Sanity assertion on the time step failed* (float32 resolution is ~1e-4 s at t ≈ 1800 s,\n   which exceeds `np.allclose`'s default tolerance). Each `jm*` directory represents one mouse. This correctly identifies all 6 subjects (jm031, jm032, jm038, jm039, jm040, jm046).\n\n## 1-c. How are the data split into sessions?\n\ni. Each session corresponds to a subdirectory within a subject's folder, sorted alphabetically. Each subdirectory contains one daily recording.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef get_sessions(base_path, subject):\n    subject_dir = os.path.join(base_path, subject)\n    sessions = [d.path for d in os.scandir(subject_dir) if d.is_dir()]\n    sessions.sort()\n    return sessions\n```\n\niii. Sorting ensures a deterministic chronological order since session folder names are date-based (`YYYY-MM-DD_a`).\n\n## 1-d. How are the data split into trials?\n\ni. Trials are defined as 60-second non-overlapping segments of the continuous recording. After 10-frame binning (30 Hz to 3 Hz), each trial is 180 bins. Any remainder bins that don't fill a complete trial are discarded.\n\nii.\n```python\ntrial_frames = TRIAL_DUR * FS // BIN_FRAMES  # 60 * 30 // 10 = 180 bins\nn_trials = n_frames // trial_frames\nremainder = n_frames - n_trials * trial_frames\n\nfor ti in range(n_trials):\n    s = ti * trial_frames\n    e = s + trial_frames\n```\n\niii. Per instruction, sessions are split into 60-second trials. The continuous recording has no natural trial structure, so fixed-length segmentation is appropriate.\n\n## 1-e. How are trials filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. No trial-level quality filtering is applied. The only data loss is discarding remainder bins at session end that don't fill a complete 60-second trial (in practice, 36000 and 54000 frames are exact multiples of 1800 frames, so nothing is discarded).\n\nii.\n```python\nif remainder > 0:\n    print(f'  session {idx}: discarding last {remainder} frames '\n          f'({remainder/FS:.1f}s) that do not fill a full trial')\n```\n\niii. No justification provided in the CONVERSION_NOTES specific to the code on disk. The CONVERSION_NOTES (which describes different code) states all 41 sessions and 1090 trials are kept.\n\n## 2-a. What variables in the raw data is the `neural` data derived from?\n\ni. Neural data is derived from two suite2p output files: `F.npy` (raw fluorescence) and `Fneu.npy` (neuropil fluorescence), both from `plane0`.\n\nii.\n```python\nF = np.load(os.path.join(session_path, 'suite2p', 'plane0', 'F.npy'))\nFneu = np.load(os.path.join(session_path, 'suite2p', 'plane0', 'Fneu.npy'))\n```\n\niii. The CONVERSION_NOTES.md states the reference code uses `F_processing` with `neucoeff=0.0` (no neuropil subtraction, so Fneu is not used). However, the actual code uses both F.npy and Fneu.npy with `NEUCOEFF=0.7`.\n\n## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. Neuropil subtraction is applied (`Fc = F - 0.7 * Fneu`), followed by suite2p's `dcnv.preprocess` function which performs baseline estimation and correction using the `maximin` method with a 60s window, sigma=10 frames, and percentile baseline of 8.\n\nii.\n```python\nNEUCOEFF = 0.7\n# ...\nFc = F - NEUCOEFF * Fneu\nFc = dcnv.preprocess(\n    F=Fc,\n    baseline='maximin',\n    win_baseline=60.0,\n    sig_baseline=10,\n    fs=FS,\n    prctile_baseline=8.0,\n    batch_size=BATCH_SIZE,\n    device=DEVICE,\n)\n```\n\niii. The CONVERSION_NOTES.md explicitly states \"No neuropil subtraction (neucoeff = 0)\" as a key decision and describes `f_processing` as a verbatim port of the track2p reference code. The actual code contradicts this by using `NEUCOEFF=0.7` and `dcnv.preprocess` instead of a direct scipy implementation. The reference code in `track2p/gui/data_management.py:F_processing` calls with no `neucoeff` argument, which defaults to 0.0.\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. No additional quality filtering is applied beyond what is already in the suite2p output. All neurons in `F.npy` are included. The code does not filter out ROIs with all-zero traces.\n\nii. (No filtering code present in convert_data.py)\n\niii. The CONVERSION_NOTES.md describes dropping 8 ROIs with identically-zero traces on at least one day (2998 to 2990 neurons), but this filtering is absent from the actual code. Suite2p's cell detection (iscell > 0.5) and Track2p tracking are already applied in the released data files.\n\n## 2-d. How is the per-trial `neural` data aligned to the event described in the `instructions`?\n\ni. Trials are aligned to the start of the session. Since trials are contiguous 60-second segments of the continuous recording with no stimulus events, no event-based alignment is needed.\n\nii.\n```python\n'metadata': {\n    'temporal_alignment_event': 'session_start',\n    'off_start': None,\n    'off_end': None,\n}\n```\n\niii. There is no stimulus event to align to. The recording is continuous spontaneous activity, and trials are artificial segments starting from the beginning of the session.\n\n## 2-e. What is the temporal resolution (time bin size) of the converted data? Is any temporal rebinning applied?\n\ni. Both the neural and the motion energy traces are averaged into non-overlapping bins of 10 consecutive frames, reducing 30 Hz to 3 Hz, i.e. a 333.33 ms time bin. Binning is applied to both streams before the motion energy is discretized.\n\nii.\n```python\nBIN_FRAMES = 10\n# ...\ndef bin_frames(x, k=BIN_FRAMES):\n    n = (x.shape[-1] // k) * k\n    x = x[..., :n]\n    return x.reshape(*x.shape[:-1], n // k, k).mean(axis=-1)\n# ...\nFc = bin_frames(Fc)\nme = bin_frames(me)\n# ...\n'metadata': {\n    'time_bin_size': BIN_FRAMES / FS * 1000,  # ms\n}\n```\n\niii. The Methods state \"for all decoding analysis we slightly denoised the dF/F as well as the behaviour traces by averaging in bins of 10 consecutive timestamps\". Both streams are binned together so they stay the same length.\n\n## 3-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Time from start of experiment* derived from?\n\ni. Time is not derived from any raw data variable. It is computed from the bin index within the session and the bin duration, giving seconds from the start of that session.\n\nii.\n```python\nt = ((s + np.arange(trial_frames)) * BIN_FRAMES / FS).astype(np.float32)\ninp_trials.append(t[np.newaxis, :])  # (1, trial_frames)\n```\n\niii. Since the frame rate is constant at 30 Hz and there are no timestamps stored with the neural data, computing time from bin indices is equivalent to using timestamps.\n\n## 3-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Time from start of experiment*?\n\ni. Time is computed as the left edge of each bin: `bin_index * 10 / 30` seconds. The first bin starts at 0.0 s. This gives a linearly increasing time vector with step size of 1/3 s.\n\nii.\n```python\n# s is the starting bin index within the session for this trial\nt = ((s + np.arange(trial_frames)) * BIN_FRAMES / FS).astype(np.float32)\n```\n\niii. The CONVERSION_NOTES.md describes using bin centres (`(bin*10 + 4.5)/30`), but the actual code computes left edges (`bin*10/30`). The first value is 0.0 s rather than 0.15 s.\n\n## 3-c. How is the `input` *Time from start of experiment* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. The time input is inherently aligned with the neural data because it is computed from the same bin indices used to slice the neural data into trials. No separate alignment step is needed.\n\nii.\n```python\n# Both use the same indices s:e\nneural_trials.append(Fc[:, s:e])\ninp_trials.append(t[np.newaxis, :])\n```\n\niii. Alignment is guaranteed by construction since both neural and time data are indexed by the same bin positions.\n\n## 4-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Motion energy* derived from?\n\ni. Motion energy is derived from `motion_energy_glob.npy` in the `move_deve` subdirectory of each session. Interframe intervals from `interframe_int.npy` are used to detect dropped camera frames.\n\nii.\n```python\nme = np.load(os.path.join(session_path, 'move_deve', 'motion_energy_glob.npy'))\ndt = np.load(os.path.join(session_path, 'move_deve', 'interframe_int.npy'))\n```\n\niii. The motion energy file contains a pre-computed global motion energy signal from the behavioral video. The interframe interval file is used to identify dropped video frames.\n\n## 4-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Motion energy*?\n\ni. Three processing steps: (1) Dropped frames are detected via interframe intervals with threshold `dt * 1000 > 0.04` and interpolated by inserting the average of neighboring values. (2) The trace is averaged into 10-frame bins. (3) The binned signal is discretized into 5 percentile-based bins whose edges are computed per session.\n\nii.\n```python\nif me.shape[0] < expected_len:\n    drop_indices = np.where(dt * 1000 > 0.04)[0]\n    for offset, idx in enumerate(drop_indices):\n        insert_pos = idx + 1 + offset\n        interp_val = (me[insert_pos - 1] + me[insert_pos]) / 2.0\n        me = np.insert(me, insert_pos, interp_val)\n\nme = bin_frames(me)\n\npercentiles = np.linspace(0, 100, n_levels + 1)\nbin_edges = np.percentile(me, percentiles)\noutput = np.digitize(me, bin_edges[1:-1])\n```\n\niii. The CONVERSION_NOTES.md describes a different approach using `tstamps.npy` to reconstruct frame indices and NaN+nanmean for missing frames. The actual code uses `interframe_int.npy` with a threshold that appears incorrect (see 4-d).\n\n## 4-c. How is `output` *Motion energy* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Per-session quintile discretization: percentile edges at [0, 20, 40, 60, 80, 100] are computed from the binned motion energy of each session, and `np.digitize` assigns each value to one of 5 bins (labels 0-4).\n\nii.\n```python\ndef discretize_motion_energy(all_me_flat, n_levels=N_LEVELS):\n    percentiles = np.linspace(0, 100, n_levels + 1)\n    all_output = []\n    all_bin_edges = []\n    for me in all_me_flat:\n        bin_edges = np.percentile(me, percentiles)\n        output = np.digitize(me, bin_edges[1:-1])  # 0-indexed levels [0, n_levels-1]\n        all_output.append(output)\n        all_bin_edges.append(bin_edges)\n    return all_output, all_bin_edges\n```\n\niii. The Decoder Task specifies \"five equal-percentile bins, selected per session\". Using `np.digitize` with the inner edges (20th, 40th, 60th, 80th percentiles) produces 5 approximately equal-sized bins.\n\n## 4-d. How is `output` *Motion energy* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. The video and neural data are acquired synchronously at 30 Hz, so they are frame-aligned in principle. Dropped camera frames (where motion energy array is shorter than neural frames) are detected using interframe intervals with threshold `dt * 1000 > 0.04` and filled by inserting interpolated values. After interpolation, an assertion checks lengths match.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef preprocess_motion_energy(session_path, expected_len):\n    me = np.load(os.path.join(session_path, 'move_deve', 'motion_energy_glob.npy'))\n    dt = np.load(os.path.join(session_path, 'move_deve', 'interframe_int.npy'))\n\n    if me.shape[0] < expected_len:\n        drop_indices = np.where(dt * 1000 > 0.04)[0]\n        n_missing = expected_len - me.shape[0]\n        for offset, idx in enumerate(drop_indices):\n            insert_pos = idx + 1 + offset\n            interp_val = (me[insert_pos - 1] + me[insert_pos]) / 2.0\n            me = np.insert(me, insert_pos, interp_val)\n\n    assert me.shape[0] == expected_len\n```\n\niii. The threshold `dt * 1000 > 0.04` is problematic. If `interframe_int.npy` values are in seconds (~0.033s at 30 Hz), then `dt * 1000` gives values in milliseconds (~33ms), and `> 0.04` (0.04 ms) would flag ALL frames as dropped. This would cause the code to insert far too many interpolated values for sessions with actual dropped frames, leading to assertion failure. For sessions without dropped frames (`me.shape[0] == expected_len`), this code path is not entered and the issue does not manifest.\n\n## 5. How are minor mistakes in the data, e.g. missing data, handled?\n\ni. Dropped video frames are detected and interpolated (with the threshold bug noted in 4-d). An assertion verifies the motion energy length matches the neural data length after interpolation. Remainder frames at the end of a session that don't fill a complete trial are discarded. The first frame of motion energy (which is always 0 as an artefact of the frame-difference definition) is NOT specially handled. All-zero neural traces are NOT removed.\n\nii.\n```python\nassert me.shape[0] == expected_len, (\n    f'motion energy length {me.shape[0]} != expected {expected_len}'\n)\n\nif remainder > 0:\n    print(f'  session {idx}: discarding last {remainder} frames ...')\n```\n\niii. The CONVERSION_NOTES.md describes setting motion energy frame 0 to NaN (artefact handling) and dropping 8 all-zero ROIs, but neither of these is present in the actual code.\n\n## 6-a. What are the most time-consuming steps of the code?\n\ni. The most time-consuming step is suite2p's `dcnv.preprocess` baseline correction, which uses GPU if available. Loading the `.npy` files is I/O bound but relatively fast.\n\nii. N/A (no explicit timing code in the AI's convert_data.py)\n\niii. The baseline correction involves sliding window operations over the full session length for every neuron. The code attempts to use GPU acceleration (`DEVICE = torch.device('cuda' if torch.cuda.is_available() else 'cpu')`).\n\n## 6-b. What loops in the code could have been vectorized to improve efficiency?\n\ni. The dropped frame interpolation loop uses `np.insert` in a for-loop, which reallocates the array each iteration. This could be vectorized by pre-allocating the output array and inserting all values at once.\n\nii.\n```python\nfor offset, idx in enumerate(drop_indices):\n    insert_pos = idx + 1 + offset\n    interp_val = (me[insert_pos - 1] + me[insert_pos]) / 2.0\n    me = np.insert(me, insert_pos, interp_val)\n```\n\niii. The number of dropped frames is typically small (0-148 per session), so the performance impact is negligible, but the repeated array reallocation is inefficient in principle.\n\n## 6-c. What processing does the code repeat multiple times?\n\ni. No significant repeated processing is identified. Each session is processed once.\n\nii. N/A\n\niii. N/A\n\n## 6-d. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Motion energy* derived from?\n\ni. Motion energy is derived from `motion_energy_glob.npy` in the `move_deve` subdirectory of each session. Interframe intervals from `interframe_int.npy` are used to detect dropped camera frames.\n\nii.\n```python\nme = np.load(os.path.join(session_path, 'move_deve', 'motion_energy_glob.npy'))\ndt = np.load(os.path.join(session_path, 'move_deve', 'interframe_int.npy'))\n```\n\niii. The motion energy file contains a pre-computed global motion energy signal from the behavioral video. The interframe interval file is used to identify dropped video frames.\n\n## 4-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Motion energy*?\n\ni. Three processing steps: (1) Dropped frames are detected via interframe intervals with threshold `dt * 1000 > 0.04` and interpolated by inserting the average of neighboring values. (2) The trace is averaged into 10-frame bins. (3) The binned signal is discretized into 5 percentile-based bins whose edges are computed per session.\n\nii.\n```python\nif me.shape[0] < expected_len:\n    drop_indices = np.where(dt * 1000 > 0.04)[0]\n    for offset, idx in enumerate(drop_indices):\n        insert_pos = idx + 1 + offset\n        interp_val = (me[insert_pos - 1] + me[insert_pos]) / 2.0\n        me = np.insert(me, insert_pos, interp_val)\n\nme = bin_frames(me)\n\npercentiles = np.linspace(0, 100, n_levels + 1)\nbin_edges = np.percentile(me, percentiles)\noutput = np.digitize(me, bin_edges[1:-1])\n```\n\niii. The CONVERSION_NOTES.md describes a different approach using `tstamps.npy` to reconstruct frame indices and NaN+nanmean for missing frames. The actual code uses `interframe_int.npy` with a threshold that appears incorrect (see 4-d).\n\n## 4-c. How is `output` *Motion energy* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Per-session quintile discretization: percentile edges at [0, 20, 40, 60, 80, 100] are computed from the binned motion energy of each session, and `np.digitize` assigns each value to one of 5 bins (labels 0-4).\n\nii.\n```python\ndef discretize_motion_energy(all_me_flat, n_levels=N_LEVELS):\n    percentiles = np.linspace(0, 100, n_levels + 1)\n    all_output = []\n    all_bin_edges = []\n    for me in all_me_flat:\n        bin_edges = np.percentile(me, percentiles)\n        output = np.digitize(me, bin_edges[1:-1])  # 0-indexed levels [0, n_levels-1]\n        all_output.append(output)\n        all_bin_edges.append(bin_edges)\n    return all_output, all_bin_edges\n```\n\niii. The Decoder Task specifies \"five equal-percentile bins, selected per session\". Using `np.digitize` with the inner edges (20th, 40th, 60th, 80th percentiles) produces 5 approximately equal-sized bins.\n\n## 4-d. How is `output` *Motion energy* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. The video and neural data are acquired synchronously at 30 Hz, so they are frame-aligned in principle. Dropped camera frames (where motion energy array is shorter than neural frames) are detected using interframe intervals with threshold `dt * 1000 > 0.04` and filled by inserting interpolated values. After interpolation, an assertion checks lengths match.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef preprocess_motion_energy(session_path, expected_len):\n    me = np.load(os.path.join(session_path, 'move_deve', 'motion_energy_glob.npy'))\n    dt = np.load(os.path.join(session_path, 'move_deve', 'interframe_int.npy'))\n\n    if me.shape[0] < expected_len:\n        drop_indices = np.where(dt * 1000 > 0.04)[0]\n        n_missing = expected_len - me.shape[0]\n        for offset, idx in enumerate(drop_indices):\n            insert_pos = idx + 1 + offset\n            interp_val = (me[insert_pos - 1] + me[insert_pos]) / 2.0\n            me = np.insert(me, insert_pos, interp_val)\n\n    assert me.shape[0] == expected_len\n```\n\niii. The threshold `dt * 1000 > 0.04` is problematic. If `interframe_int.npy` values are in seconds (~0.033s at 30 Hz), then `dt * 1000` gives values in milliseconds (~33ms), and `> 0.04` (0.04 ms) would flag ALL frames as dropped. This would cause the code to insert far too many interpolated values for sessions with actual dropped frames, leading to assertion failure. For sessions without dropped frames (`me.shape[0] == expected_len`), this code path is not entered and the issue does not manifest.\n\n## 5. How are minor mistakes in the data, e.g. missing data, handled?\n\ni. Dropped video frames are detected and interpolated (with the threshold bug noted in 4-d). An assertion verifies the motion energy length matches the neural data length after interpolation. Remainder frames at the end of a session that don't fill a complete trial are discarded. The first frame of motion energy (which is always 0 as an artefact of the frame-difference definition) is NOT specially handled. All-zero neural traces are NOT removed.\n\nii.\n```python\nassert me.shape[0] == expected_len, (\n    f'motion energy length {me.shape[0]} != expected {expected_len}'\n)\n\nif remainder > 0:\n    print(f'  session {idx}: discarding last {remainder} frames ...')\n```\n\niii. The CONVERSION_NOTES.md describes setting motion energy frame 0 to NaN (artefact handling) and dropping 8 all-zero ROIs, but neither of these is present in the actual code.\n\n## 6-a. What are the most time-consuming steps of the code?\n\ni. The most time-consuming step is suite2p's `dcnv.preprocess` baseline correction, which uses GPU if available. Loading the `.npy` files is I/O bound but relatively fast.\n\nii. N/A (no explicit timing code in the AI's convert_data.py)\n\niii. The baseline correction involves sliding window operations over the full session length for every neuron. The code attempts to use GPU acceleration (`DEVICE = torch.device('cuda' if torch.cuda.is_available() else 'cpu')`).\n\n## 6-b. What loops in the code could have been vectorized to improve efficiency?\n\ni. The dropped frame interpolation loop uses `np.insert` in a for-loop, which reallocates the array each iteration. This could be vectorized by pre-allocating the output array and inserting all values at once.\n\nii.\n```python\nfor offset, idx in enumerate(drop_indices):\n    insert_pos = idx + 1 + offset\n    interp_val = (me[insert_pos - 1] + me[insert_pos]) / 2.0\n    me = np.insert(me, insert_pos, interp_val)\n```\n\niii. The number of dropped frames is typically small (0-148 per session), so the performance impact is negligible, but the repeated array reallocation is inefficient in principle.\n\n## 6-c. What processing does the code repeat multiple times?\n\ni. No significant repeated processing is identified. Each session is processed once.\n\nii. N/A\n\niii. N/A\n\n## 6-d. Given that the reference code does not perform neuropil subtraction (neucoeff=0), loading Fneu.npy is unnecessary additional processing.\n\nii.\n```python\nFneu = np.load(os.path.join(session_path, 'suite2p', 'plane0', 'Fneu.npy'))\nFc = F - NEUCOEFF * Fneu\n```\n\niii. The reference code's `F_processing` uses `neucoeff=0.0` by default, meaning Fneu is never used.
